March 2023: Kentucky Power filed its 2022 integrated resource plan with the Public Service Commission, and the preferred plan adds 480 MW of new gas combustion turbines in 2029. This is the first document that announces the unit. https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2023-00092/sebishop@aep.com/03202023030104/KPCO_2022_IRP_Volume_A-Public.pdf Kentucky Power told the Public Service Commission in October 2026 that it dropped its plan for a 450 MW combustion turbine (case 2026-00103, withdrawn by the order of October 29, 2026) and will instead seek approval for a 760 MW combined cycle plant in Lawrence County with a planned in-service date of March 31, 2031 (new case 2026-00324, notice of intent filed October 30, 2026).

### G100001071840 / BREC 2023 IRP power station / 1 IRP
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only))*
- about: validation
- question: When did this unit reach the announced step?
- finding: Big Rivers submitted its 2023 integrated resource plan to the Commission on September 29, 2023. The Commission staff report dated August 20, 2024 (https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2023%20Cases/2023-00310/20240820_PSC_ORDER.pdf) says so and describes the 635 MW combined cycle for 2029 in that plan.
- recommendation: Date the announced step September 2023, with the staff report as the document.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2023%20Cases/2023-00310/20240820_PSC_ORDER.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The plan itself is case 2023-00310 on psc.ky.gov. The staff report is the document that I read and checked.

- notes: The 2026 plan case 2026-00167 (https://psc.ky.gov/Case/ViewCaseFilings/2026-00167) has no plan filed yet. Pass three: the step is now dated as an edit to Status Detail with a June 2024 milestone from the same plan.

### G100001055334 / East Bend Generating Station / Unit 2, timepoint 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed), 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 38 (GEM IDs matched to EIA-860M, EIP and Sierra Club (US only))*
- about: Status Detail
- question: Does the federal funding note for East Bend unit 2 apply to the conversion?
- finding: The Department of Energy page (https://www.energy.gov/hgeo/defense-production-act-title-iii-project-selections-coal-fleet-projects-and-west-gateway) lists 33,400,000 dollars for the East Bend Coal Reliability Assurance Initiative. It covers steam turbine and boiler economizer work to keep the coal unit running. It does not mention diesel or gas.
- recommendation: Keep the note but add that the funding is for coal reliability work, not the conversion.
  - links: https://www.energy.gov/hgeo/defense-production-act-title-iii-project-selections-coal-fleet-projects-and-west-gateway (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The note is a running log, so a person adds the clarifying line above the old text.

### G100001055334 / East Bend Generating Station / Unit 2, timepoint 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only))*
- about: validation
- question: When did East Bend unit 2 reach the pre-construction step?
- finding: I found no permit application or construction filing for the diesel conversion. The earliest document is the 2024 Duke Energy Kentucky plan filed in June 2024 (https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2024-00197/e.rolfes-adkins%40duke-energy.com/09092024025857/DEK_IRP_PUBLIC_06212024.pdf), which only proposes the conversion.
- recommendation: Not found. The plan alone supports announced, so consider whether pre-construction is right; leave the step undated.

- notes: I looked at the Kentucky Public Service Commission cases for Duke, the harvested air permit index and Boone County minutes. Pass three: the step is now dated as an edit to Status Detail with a June 2024 milestone from the same plan.

### G100001096399 / Elmer Smith Station / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: validation
- question: When did Unit 1 reach the announced step?
- finding: The first report I found is The Owensboro Times article of June 17, 2026. It says Green River East GenCo LLC has applied to connect a 545 MW plant and that the utility had earlier approved an option to lease.
- recommendation: Date the announced step June 2026. The option-to-lease vote has no published date, so June 2026 is the earliest date found; it is proposed as a Status Detail edit with a dated milestone.
  - links: https://www.owensborotimes.com/news/2026/06/omu-exploring-545-megawatt-natural-gas-power-plant-at-former-elmer-smith-site/ (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.daviessky.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/OB-43-08-13-2026-Minutes.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: I searched the Midcontinent Independent System Operator queue file at https://www.misoenergy.org/api/giqueue/getprojects. It has no row for Elmer Smith or Green River East, which fits the article saying the interconnection study is run by Owensboro Municipal Utilities and not yet by the grid operator. The Daviess County Fiscal Court minutes of August 13, 2026 at https://www.daviessky.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/OB-43-08-13-2026-Minutes.pdf mention the proposed 545 MW plant in public comment but give no date for the announcement. I could not find the date of the option-to-lease vote or a Kentucky Secretary of State record. Third pass: the date is now also proposed as a milestone on the Status Detail finding.

### G100001096399 / Elmer Smith Station / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed), 25 (Location data for every entry)*
- about: Location accuracy
- question: Should the Elmer Smith point stay exact?
- finding: The permit address and a satellite look both put GEM's point on the former Elmer Smith plant pad. The leased 30 acre parcel is not published.
- recommendation: Leave the point and accuracy as they are.
  - links: https://www.owensborotimes.com/news/2026/06/omu-exploring-545-megawatt-natural-gas-power-plant-at-former-elmer-smith-site/ (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: I did not run a satellite look. No document gives coordinates, and the Owensboro Times article at https://www.owensborotimes.com/news/2026/06/omu-exploring-545-megawatt-natural-gas-power-plant-at-former-elmer-smith-site/ names only the former plant site and about 30 acres.

### G100000403648 / Marshall energy facility / CT4
*question about existing data; checklist rows 7 (Validation report errors fixed)*
- about: validation
- question: What year did CT4 at Marshall reach mothballed status?
- finding: I found no TVA board action, plan or filing that dates a mothballing of CT4. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) lists CT4 with status standby, available for service but not normally used, and an operating date in 2002. The 2026 TVA integrated resource plan, volume 2 (https://tva-azr-eastus-cdn-ep-tvawcm-prd.azureedge.net/cdn-tvawcma/docs/default-source/environment/environmental-stewardship/integrated-resource-plan/2026-irp/prelimfinal-2026-irp-vol-2-eis_20260618_r1.pdf?sfvrsn=208e1a89_3), lists Marshall as an existing plant of 8 units with no retirement or new unit.
- recommendation: Leave the mothball date blank and add no retired year. Ask TVA for the date if the database needs one.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://tva-azr-eastus-cdn-ep-tvawcm-prd.azureedge.net/cdn-tvawcma/docs/default-source/environment/environmental-stewardship/integrated-resource-plan/2026-irp/prelimfinal-2026-irp-vol-2-eis_20260618_r1.pdf?sfvrsn=208e1a89_3 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: I searched the EIA table, the TVA 2026 integrated resource plan volumes 1 and 2, the Kentucky air permit from February 2023 and a web search. The TVA plan page is at https://www.tva.com/environment/2026-integrated-resource-plan. TVA is outside Kentucky Public Service Commission jurisdiction, and no commission case matched. In the third pass I also read the TVA Marshall plant page at https://www.tva.com/energy/our-power-system/natural-gas/marshall-combustion-turbine-plant and a January 2018 WCBL article at https://www.marshallcountydaily.com/2018/01/10/combustion-turbine-plant-keeps-tva-power-going-strong/. Neither says when any unit was mothballed. A web search for the mothballing date also found nothing from TVA. Board minutes were not searched.

*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 17 (Unknown technology rechecked)*
**Action:** Set Turbine/Engine Technology of unit BSG1 (G100000403163) to aeroderivative gas turbine. It now says gas turbine. Add the link below to the Turbine/Engine Technology Data Source box. Keep the links already there.
- `Turbine/Engine Technology`: `gas turbine` -> `aeroderivative gas turbine`
  - Turbine/Engine Technology Data Source: https://web.archive.org/web/20250307233856/https://dep.wv.gov/daq/permitting/titlevpermits/Documents/October%202024/099-00080/Final%20Pemit%20R30-09900080-2024.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: high (independent: False)
- notes: The permit names a Pratt & Whitney FT8 Twin Pac. The FT8 is an aeroderivative turbine, but the permit does not use that word, so this rests on the model name. GEM now says gas turbine.

*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 16 (Zero-capacity units rechecked)*

A Twin Pac is two gas generators on one set. This is a saved copy from the Wayback Machine.

It describes a natural gas fired combined cycle unit and lists a diesel emergency generator of 2000 kilowatts and a diesel fire pump on the same permit. The diesel is burned in those small engines, not in the turbine, so the permit only partly supports the diesel entry. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 lists natural gas only for generator 5.

### G100000403163 / Big Sandy Peaker Plant / BSG1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 11 (Every unit has a unit name)*
- about: identity
- question: Is the Big Sandy Peaker Plant in West Virginia, not Kentucky?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) lists plant 55284 in Wayne County, West Virginia. The West Virginia air permit R30-09900080 from October 2024 (https://web.archive.org/web/20250307233856/https://dep.wv.gov/daq/permitting/titlevpermits/Documents/October%202024/099-00080/Final%20Pemit%20R30-09900080-2024.pdf) gives the facility location as Kenova, Wayne County, West Virginia. GEM shows the state as Kentucky.
- recommendation: Change State/Province to West Virginia for all six units in the web form. This moves the plant out of the Kentucky assignment.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://web.archive.org/web/20250307233856/https://dep.wv.gov/daq/permitting/titlevpermits/Documents/October%202024/099-00080/Final%20Pemit%20R30-09900080-2024.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The permit address is a saved copy from the Wayback Machine because the live address returns an error. Coordinates 38.3441, -82.5938 sit at Kenova on the Ohio River, which fits West Virginia. I did not change Latitude or Longitude.

### G100000402793 / Bluegrass generating station / CT1
*question about existing data*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should Bluegrass capacity stay at 208 MW per unit or follow EIA at 193.8 MW?
- finding: The draft Title V permit (https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/14642064) says each turbine can generate 208 MW. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) lists 193.8 MW nameplate for each of CT1, CT2 and CT3.
- recommendation: Change Capacity (MW) to 193.8 for each of the three units, since the EIA figure is the only one labeled as nameplate. If the reviewer prefers the permit rating, leave 208 MW as is.
  - links: https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/14642064 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2025%20Cases/2025-00087//20260601_PSC_ORDER.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: I searched for the reason for the gap. The Kentucky Division for Air Quality draft Title V permit (https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/14642064) gives a maximum continuous rating of 208 MW per turbine at the permit's rated heat input. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) gives a nameplate of 193.8 MW, a summer net of 165 MW and a winter net of 192 MW per unit. The Kentucky Public Service Commission staff report on the East Kentucky Power Cooperative 2025 resource plan (https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2025%20Cases/2025-00087//20260601_PSC_ORDER.pdf) gives 167 MW summer and 189 MW winter per turbine. No document I found gives a generator nameplate plate figure of 208 MW. The 208 MW figure is a permit rating, so it may be a maximum rather than the nameplate. EIA is the only source that labels its figure as nameplate.

### G100001031363 / KYMEA Energy Center I / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 11 (Every unit has a unit name)*
- about: identity
- question: Is MISO queue row S1073 the interconnection request for KYMEA Energy Center I?
- finding: Row S1073 is 75 MW with no fuel shown, queued February 19, 2025, at the Hopkins to Reid 161 kV line owned by Big Rivers, with Webster County listed. The size and the Big Rivers connection fit the plant. The queue does not name KYMEA, and the KYMEA page says its switching station was transferred to LG&E and KU, whose area EIA lists as the balancing authority.
- recommendation: Treat S1073 as a probable match but do not cite it for any value. Ask KYMEA or check the interconnection agreement if a firm link is needed.
- notes: I read the MISO project list file on November 5, 2026. Row J3089 is a withdrawn 696 MW Reid combined cycle and is unrelated. The queue page itself is a lead and was not run through the verifier.

### G100000403359 / PPS power plant No 1 / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 17 (Unknown technology rechecked)*
- about: Turbine/Engine Technology
- question: Should the turbine type for both units be aeroderivative gas turbine?
- finding: The 2024 Kentucky air permit (https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/12647679) names Pratt and Whitney FT8-3 SwiftPac 60 packages. The permit counts four turbines in two pairs, one pair for each tracker unit. The tracker shows gas turbine.
- recommendation: Change the technology to aeroderivative gas turbine for both units, and consider setting Number Of Engines to 2 and Capacity Per Engine to about 30 MW.
  - links: https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/12647679 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: I did not change the technology because the brief did not ask for a technology check. The per-turbine rating is not stated in the permit. Only the pair output of 60.3 MW is.

### Paducah American Energy Hub gas plant
*question about existing data; checklist rows 25 (Location data for every entry)*
- about: location
- question: Should the Paducah American Energy Hub gas plant stay at the former Paducah Gaseous Diffusion Plant point?
- finding: NextEra's fact sheet and the Kentucky Lantern say the gas plants will be built at or near the former Paducah plant in McCracken County. No permit or site plan gives a final point yet.
- recommendation: Keep the point as approximate at the former plant site and move it when an air permit application gives the site.

- notes: The fact sheet also lists up to 2.6 gigawatts of battery storage, which is not in scope. The fact sheet says the data center could reach 8 gigawatts.

### G100000410525 / Paradise Fossil Plant / 4
*question about existing data*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should the three Paradise gas turbines be 231.2 MW instead of 250 MW?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) gives 231.2 MW nameplate for each of the three simple cycle turbines CT5, CT6 and CT7. GEM shows 250 MW each, which matches the TVA environmental review figure. The Kentucky air permit record lists 229 MW at 59 degrees for these turbines.
- recommendation: Check the Tennessee Valley Authority nameplate or the generator data sheet. If it confirms 231.2 MW, change units 4, 5 and 6 to 231.2 MW.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/14331535 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.tva.com/energy/our-power-system/natural-gas/paradise-combined-cycle-plant (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The Kentucky draft Title V air permit V-26-011 (https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/14331535) rates each of these turbines at 229 MW at 59 degrees Fahrenheit and the Tennessee Valley Authority plant page (https://www.tva.com/energy/our-power-system/natural-gas/paradise-combined-cycle-plant) says three turbines add 681 MW, which is 227 MW each. The 250 MW figure in GEM matches the environmental review. The combined cycle block matches the EIA total of 1,160 MW.

### G100000403452 / Riverside Generating (KY) power station / GTG1
*question about existing data*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Do the PJM Riverside uprate requests W3-162 and Y2-045 change any unit capacity?
- finding: The PJM queue file at https://www.pjm.com/pub/planning/downloads/xml/PlanningQueues.xml lists request W3-162 for 20 MW, in service September 2011, and Y2-045 for 6 MW, in service January 2016. Both name Riverside Generating at Baker 345 kV and show a facility output of 993 and 999 MW. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 still lists 230 MW for each of the five units.
- recommendation: Leave unit capacity at 230 MW. Ask the project manager whether the 26 MW of added output should be recorded.
  - links: https://www.pjm.com/pub/planning/downloads/xml/PlanningQueues.xml (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The queue file does not say which turbine each uprate belongs to. Requests W1-035 (750 MW, withdrawn) and X2-006 (no capacity) are earlier Riverside expansion requests that never entered service.

## Checklist group 3. Status and timeline

Status changes, start and retirement years, planned retirements, Latest Activity.

### G100001048351 / Cooper power station / Unit 2, timepoint 2
*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 38 (GEM IDs matched to EIA-860M, EIP and Sierra Club (US only))*
**Action:** Add the entry below at the top of the Status Detail box of unit Unit 2, timepoint 2 (G100001048351), above the text already there. Keep the existing text below it, word for word. The source links are part of the entry, one per line. Do not add them to the Status Data Source box.

The Public Service Commission quarterly report filed October 30, 2026 shows engineering is under way, the gas firing system is ordered, the general contractor is to be chosen in early 2027, construction starts in January 2028, and completion is December 2029. The Department of Energy awarded $19,326,832 for this co-firing project, agreement signed September 17, 2026.

### G100001018034 / Green Station / Unit 1, timepoint 2
*change to an existing unit*
**Action:** Add the entry below at the top of the Status Detail box of unit Unit 1, timepoint 2 (G100001018034), above the text already there. Keep the existing text below it, word for word. The source links are part of the entry, one per line. Do not add them to the Status Data Source box.

Big Rivers filed its 2026 integrated resource plan on September 29, 2026. The preferred plan keeps both Sebree units in service through 2050 and drops the 2029 retirement and replacement from the 2023 plan. The plan also proposes about four million gallons of on-site fuel oil storage for Sebree units 1 and 2, giving up to 96 hours of backup fuel, with an in-service date of January 2029.

https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf
https://psc.ky.gov/Case/ViewCaseFilings/2026-00291
- `Status Detail`: `NF 5/21/25: Potential retirement in 2029 and replacement it a 635 MW BREC 2023 IRP power station (preferred plan). 
NF: Converted to gas in 2022.` -> `Big Rivers filed its 2026 integrated resource plan on September 29, 2026. The preferred plan keeps both Sebree units in service through 2050 and drops the 2029 retirement and replacement from the 2023 plan. The plan also proposes about four million gallons of on-site fuel oil storage for Sebree units 1 and 2, giving up to 96 hours of backup fuel, with an in-service date of January 2029.

https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf
https://psc.ky.gov/Case/ViewCaseFilings/2026-00291

NF 5/21/25: Potential retirement in 2029 and replacement it a 635 MW BREC 2023 IRP power station (preferred plan). 
NF: Converted to gas in 2022.`
  - Status Detail: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - Status Detail: https://psc.ky.gov/Case/ViewCaseFilings/2026-00291 (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: high (independent: False)
- notes: The 2026 plan at https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf says the plant stays in service through 2050 in the base case and describes the fuel oil backup project. The Kentucky Public Service Commission case page at https://psc.ky.gov/Case/ViewCaseFilings/2026-00291 shows Big Rivers filed case 2026-00291 in September 2026 asking for a certificate for backup fuel capability at the Sebree station. Only a notice of intent has been filed there so far, so the fuel and cost come from the plan.

Reid combustion turbine, about 65 MW net, in service since 1976, and says Big Rivers proposes to retire it because of the unit's catastrophic failure. ### G100001071840 / BREC 2023 IRP power station / 1 IRP
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 20 (No start year on cancelled or shelved projects), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Status
- question: Should this 635 MW placeholder be cancelled now that the 2026 plan drops it?
- finding: Big Rivers filed its 2026 integrated resource plan on September 29, 2026 (https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf). Its preferred portfolio on pages 281 to 283 keeps the Sebree coal units running and adds no combined cycle plant. The plan says capacity additions are only considered after 2031.
- recommendation: Change Status to cancelled and add Cancellation year 2026, with the 2026 plan as the source. Add a Status Detail line above the existing text saying the 2026 plan dropped the project.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2023%20Cases/2023-00310/20240820_PSC_ORDER.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: This is a single-source status change, so a second source would confirm it, for example a Big Rivers press release or board materials. The 2023 plan, as summarized in the Commission staff report of August 20, 2024 (https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2023%20Cases/2023-00310/20240820_PSC_ORDER.pdf), had the Sebree units retiring in 2029 and a 635 MW combined cycle added that year. The Start year of 2029 should be cleared once the status is cancelled. The 2026 action plan also seeks approval to retire the small Reid combustion turbine and add fuel oil backup at the Sebree station. Latest Activity is left alone because the newest report is under a year old.

### G100001048350 / Cooper power station / Unit 3
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 15 (Turbine make and model searched for in-development units)*
- about: Equipment Manufacturer/Model
- question: Should Unit 3's turbine maker be Siemens Energy, not GE Vernova?
- finding: The air permit statement of basis lists the combustion turbines as Siemens 5000F. GEM shows GE Vernova: SGT6-5000F, and SGT6-5000F is a Siemens Energy model.
- recommendation: Change the maker to Siemens Energy and keep SGT6-5000F as the model.
  - links: https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/14648293 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: I could not find a document that says SGT6-5000F in so many words.

### G100000413046 / E.W. Brown Generating Station / 12
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 38 (GEM IDs matched to EIA-860M, EIP and Sierra Club (US only))*
- about: Start year

### G100001055334 / East Bend Generating Station / Unit 2, timepoint 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 21 (Planned retirements searched), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only))*
- about: Planned retire
- question: Should East Bend unit 2 planned retirement be 2039 instead of 2038?
- finding: The 2024 Duke Energy Kentucky plan (https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2024-00197/e.rolfes-adkins%40duke-energy.com/09092024025857/DEK_IRP_PUBLIC_06212024.pdf) says dual fuel operation from 2030 and a combined cycle replacement by 2039. A table in the plan reads retire in 2039 for the co-firing option. I found no document that says 2038.
- recommendation: Check the source of 2038. If none exists, change Planned retire to 2039 and cite the 2024 plan.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2024-00197/e.rolfes-adkins%40duke-energy.com/09092024025857/DEK_IRP_PUBLIC_06212024.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The plan also studies retiring in 2032 or 2036 in other cases, but the preferred portfolio keeps the unit to 2039.

### G100001096399 / Elmer Smith Station / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 8 (No blanks for fuel, status, technology, country, coordinates, accuracy, owner), 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 17 (Unknown technology rechecked)*
- about: Turbine/Engine Technology
- question: What technology will the Elmer Smith plant use?
- finding: No article, permit or queue row found names a turbine type, model or maker. No air permit application exists in the state eSearch record, which only holds the old coal plant permits.
- recommendation: Leave technology as unknown until an air permit application or interconnection record names it.
  - links: https://www.owensborotimes.com/news/2026/06/omu-exploring-545-megawatt-natural-gas-power-plant-at-former-elmer-smith-site/ (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The Kentucky eSearch air permit record at https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch/ holds only the old coal plant permits. The Midcontinent queue file has no row for this project. No turbine maker or type is named in the June 2026 articles or the August 2026 county minutes.

### G100001018034 / Green Station / Unit 1, timepoint 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 21 (Planned retirements searched), 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Planned retire
- question: Should the 2029 planned retirement of Sebree units 1 and 2 be removed?
- finding: Big Rivers' 2026 integrated resource plan keeps both Sebree units in service through 2050 in the base case. Its only retirement case for the plant is a test case in 2039. The 2023 plan's 2029 retirement and 635 MW combined cycle replacement is not in the 2026 plan.
- recommendation: Clear Planned retire on both units, and add a Status Detail entry saying the 2026 plan keeps the plant running. Do not add the combined cycle at the Sebree station.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Source is the 2026 plan at https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf. I found no combined cycle sited at the Sebree station in it. Same applies to unit G100001018035. A person should remove the old scheduled retirement event. Both units are covered by this question.

### G100001030601 / Liberty Station Power Plant / 1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 10 (Every unit in development updated, with a Last Updated entry), 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 38 (GEM IDs matched to EIA-860M, EIP and Sierra Club (US only)), 49 (Construction to operating checked), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Status
- question: Should Liberty Station stay at construction when EIA lists it as planned?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 lists all 12 engines as planned with regulatory approvals not initiated, though the commission certificate was granted May 20, 2025 and the air permit was issued March 6, 2026. EKPC's own filings give planned dates for site work and a May 20, 2026 groundbreaking, and the June 2026 report shows construction start at 0 percent.
- recommendation: Leave the status as construction. Check EKPC's next semi-annual report, due about December 2026, for the actual construction start.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2024-00310/greg.cecil@ekpc.coop/03202026030536/Closed/Letter_Liberty_RICE__construction_commencement.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)

- notes: I found a May 2026 press report of the groundbreaking at https://marcellusdrilling.com/?p=119142 and a trade piece at https://www.electric.coop/ekpcs-new-gas-plant-shows-strong-investment-in-rural-kentucky-community but no independent dated report of work under way. EIA is likely just behind the permits.

- what to watch: PJM cycle row C01-1227 shows East Kentucky Power Cooperative requesting 758 MW of gas at Spurlock, but no certificate, air permit or county record names it, and the row cannot be fetched by a plain address.

### G100001018034 / Green Station / Unit 1, timepoint 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 13 (Coal-to-gas conversions have gas fuel and technology)*
- about: Fuel
- question: Should fuel oil be added to the Sebree units once the backup fuel case is decided?
- finding: Big Rivers' 2026 plan proposes fuel oil backup for Sebree units 1 and 2, in service January 2029. Case 2026-00291 at https://psc.ky.gov/Case/ViewCaseFilings/2026-00291 has only a notice of intent so far, with no application or order.
- recommendation: Leave Fuel as fossil gas for now and recheck when the commission rules on case 2026-00291.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/Case/ViewCaseFilings/2026-00291 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Same applies to unit G100001018035. The cost is redacted in the public plan.

### G100000413034 / Mill Creek Station / MC 5
*question about existing data; checklist rows 13 (Coal-to-gas conversions have gas fuel and technology)*
- about: Fuel
- question: Should diesel stay in the fuel entry for Mill Creek units 5 and 6?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 lists natural gas only for unit 5. The air permits show diesel only in emergency generators and fire pumps, not in the turbines.
- recommendation: Ask the project manager whether diesel belongs in the fuel entry. If the turbines have no diesel backup, change the fuel to natural gas only.
  - links: https://web.archive.org/web/20260516170502/https://louisvilleky.gov/sites/default/files/2026-05/C-0127-22-0046-V%20(R2)_Draft.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://web.archive.org/web/2026/https://louisvilleky.gov/sites/default/files/2026-05/C-0127-25-0007-V_Draft.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Sources: https://web.archive.org/web/20260516170502/https://louisvilleky.gov/sites/default/files/2026-05/C-0127-22-0046-V%20(R2)_Draft.pdf and https://web.archive.org/web/2026/https://louisvilleky.gov/sites/default/files/2026-05/C-0127-25-0007-V_Draft.pdf. I found no document that says the turbines can burn diesel.

### G100000402523 / Robert Reid Power Plant / Reid 1, timepoint 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 13 (Coal-to-gas conversions have gas fuel and technology)*
- about: Conversion/replacement?
- question: Is Reid 1 timepoint 2 really a coal-to-gas conversion started in 2015?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx shows no gas generator 1. It shows the old coal unit 1, 96 MW, retired in September 2020, and a separate gas combustion turbine, generator GEN2, 98.8 MW, operating since March 1976. GEM's 99 MW gas unit matches GEN2.
- recommendation: Ask the person who marked it a conversion for their source. If none exists, treat this unit as the 1976 combustion turbine, clear the conversion label and the 2015 start year, and use GEN2 as the unit ID in Other IDs (unit).
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: I found no document that states a gas conversion of Reid in 2015, so I could not fill Conversion/replacement Data Source, Backup Power Data Source or a start year source. The existing start year source is a news article about the implosion of idled units at Sebree. The county should also be filled as Webster, which the EIA table gives for plant 1383. County and the generator ID are values for a person to type into the web form.

## Checklist group 5. Turbine make and model

Equipment manufacturer and model, especially on units in development.

### G100000403163 / Big Sandy Peaker Plant / BSG1
*change to an existing unit*
**Action:** Set Equipment Manufacturer/Model of unit BSG1 (G100000403163) to Pratt & Whitney FT8 Twin Pac. The box is empty now. Add the link below to the Turbine/Engine Equipment Data Source box. Keep the links already there.
- `Equipment Manufacturer/Model`: `` -> `Pratt & Whitney FT8 Twin Pac`
  - Turbine/Engine Equipment Data Source: https://web.archive.org/web/20250307233856/https://dep.wv.gov/daq/permitting/titlevpermits/Documents/October%202024/099-00080/Final%20Pemit%20R30-09900080-2024.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: high (independent: False)
- notes: The West Virginia Division of Air Quality Title V permit R30-09900080 from October 2024 (https://dep.wv.gov/daq/permitting/titlevpermits/Documents/October%202024/099-00080/Final%20Pemit%20R30-09900080-2024.pdf) lists emission unit GS-01 as a Pratt & Whitney FT8 Twin Pac turbine, 599.02 million Btu per hour, installed 2000. The live address now returns an error, so this is a saved copy from the Wayback Machine. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) shows plant 55284, generator BSG1, operating, 58.9 MW nameplate, natural gas, combustion turbine, operating July 2001, no planned retirement. This confirms the current status, capacity, fuel and start year. The permit lists two engines per unit, so engine count and capacity per engine are now filled. No retirement, mothballing or planned retirement was found: the EIA-860M table shows no planned retirement, and the September 2026 Indiana order describes the plant as operating since 2001. Operator, Latest Activity, Status Detail and Conversion/replacement were not filled because no document gave a new value and the unit is operating and not converted.

### G100000401618 / Cane Run Station / CR7
*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only)), 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
**Action:** Leave Status of unit CR7 (G100000401618) as operating. It is still correct. Add the 3 links below to the Status Data Source box. Keep the links already there.
- `Status`: `operating` -> `operating`
  - Status Data Source: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - Status Data Source: https://louisvilleky.gov/sites/default/files/2025-11/20251113-basis-O-0126-25-V.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
  - Status Data Source: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2024-00326/rick.lovekamp%40lge-ku.com/10182024014139/06-LGE_KU_2024_IRP_Volume_I.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: high (independent: True)
- notes: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) lists plant 1363 generators 7A, 7B and 7S as operating, with no planned retirement. The Louisville Air Pollution Control District statement of basis from November 2025 (https://louisvilleky.gov/sites/default/files/2025-11/20251113-basis-O-0126-25-V.pdf) describes the combined cycle unit as an active permitted source. The status is unchanged, so this re-verifies it. The 2024 integrated resource plan for the two utilities (https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2024-00326/rick.lovekamp%40lge-ku.com/10182024014139/06-LGE_KU_2024_IRP_Volume_I.pdf) lists Cane Run 7 as an existing unit that began operation in 2015, with no retirement planned. The neighboring coal unit, Cane Run 3, is not part of this plant row. Unit CR7 is still operating in the EIA-860M table for September 2026 with no planned retirement. The 2024 integrated resource plan lists no retirement or mothballing, and shows only a capacity uprate assumed for 2026. No change of owner was found. I read the two Kentucky Public Service Commission cases the harvest flagged. Case 2022-00098 is the East Kentucky Power Cooperative plan and has nothing on this plant. Case 2023-00122 is the retirement application for other LG&E and KU units, and its application does not name Cane Run.

### G100001018034 / Green Station / Unit 1, timepoint 2
*change to an existing unit; checklist rows 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
**Action:** Leave Status of unit Unit 1, timepoint 2 (G100001018034) as operating. It is still correct. Add the link below to the Status Data Source box. Keep the links already there.
- `Status`: `operating` -> `operating`
  - Status Data Source: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: high (independent: False)
- notes: The Big Rivers 2026 integrated resource plan at https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf describes both Sebree units as existing gas-fired units that stay in service. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 also lists both as operating. No retirement, mothballing or owner change was found. A Power Engineering article from August 2022 at https://www.power-eng.com/gas/coal-to-gas-conversion-completed-at-500-mw-kentucky-plant/ says the plant now burns natural gas after the conversion. Both units were converted from coal to gas in 2022, so the start year of 2022 stands as the conversion year. The EIA-860M table lists 1979 and 1981 as the original operating years. The 2029 planned retirement no longer matches the 2026 plan, see the question raised. Latest Activity is left blank on purpose because the unit is operating and the field applies only to units in development, shelved units or units with an inferred status.

### G100000403163 / Big Sandy Peaker Plant / BSG1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 19 (Missing or unknown owner searched), 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Owner(s)
- question: Has Indiana Michigan Power bought the Big Sandy Peaker Plant from Middle River Power II?
- finding: The Indiana Utility Regulatory Commission order of September 2, 2026 (https://secure.in.gov/iurc/files/ord_46301GRS1_090226.pdf) approves Indiana Michigan Power's request to buy the Big Sandy Peaker Plant in Kenova, West Virginia, for $299,525,000. The same order says closing still needs federal approvals from FERC and under the Hart-Scott-Rodino Act. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 already names Indiana Michigan Power Co, but I found no closing notice.
- recommendation: Leave Owner(s) as Middle River Power II LLC [100%] for now. Add the approved but not yet closed purchase to Notes and the possible-updates sheet, then look for a closing notice.
  - links: https://secure.in.gov/iurc/files/ord_46301GRS1_090226.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.journalgazette.net/local/indiana-michigan-power-seeks-permission-to-buy-west-virginia-natural-gas-plant/article_a6f2d661-c888-46b0-a052-94c8259c0170.html (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: An owner change needs a closed sale from two independent publishers. I found neither. The permit lists Big Sandy Peaker Plant, LLC as the permittee but I could not read its parent. The owner's own page and a 2025 or 2026 filing naming Middle River Power II as owner were not found in the budget I had. The order names the seller only as the seller, so Middle River Power II and Partners Group are not confirmed by it.

### G100000402523 / Robert Reid Power Plant / Reid 1, timepoint 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 43 (Ownership changes for operating units reviewed (optional)), 50 (Other last-minute status changes checked)*
- about: Status
- question: Should Reid 1 still be marked operating when it has been out since August 2024?
- finding: Big Rivers' 2026 resource plan at https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf says the Reid combustion turbine has been unavailable since a catastrophic failure on August 24, 2024. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 at https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx still lists it as operating. Big Rivers filed notice on September 24, 2026 that it will ask to retire the unit.
- recommendation: Leave the status as operating for now. Re-check when the commission rules on case 2026-00291 and move the unit to retired once a document gives the retirement date.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00291/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09242026042139/Notice_of_Intent_to_File_Application.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2026%20cases/2026-00291//20260924_Big%20Rivers%20Electric%20Corporation%20Notice%20of%20Intent%20and%20Election.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The notice at https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00291/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09242026042139/Notice_of_Intent_to_File_Application.pdf gives no retirement date. A second filing, the notice of election at https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2026%20cases/2026-00291//20260924_Big%20Rivers%20Electric%20Corporation%20Notice%20of%20Intent%20and%20Election.pdf, says Big Rivers will file the application no later than November 20, 2026. The case page https://psc.ky.gov/Case/ViewCaseFilings/2026-00291 listed no application on November 5, 2026. So I did not propose a planned retirement year. Neither the notice nor the 2026 resource plan states a year. No mothballed status was found in any document.

  - Start Year Data Source: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2025-00175/mmcaldwell@aep.com/06302025034730/02_KPCO_Application.pdf (ok=True, contains_value=True)
- confidence: high (independent: False)
- notes: A Kentucky Power application to the Kentucky Public Service Commission from June 2025 in case 2025-00175 (https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2025-00175/mmcaldwell@aep.com/06302025034730/02_KPCO_Application.pdf) says Big Sandy Unit 1 was converted to natural gas and returned to service on May 31, 2016. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) shows 1963, which is the year the original coal unit started. GEM's 2016 is the conversion year and is right for this row. The unit is still operating. EIA lists no planned retirement date, so the 2041 date in GEM rests on the Kentucky Power integrated resource plan already cited, and I did not find a newer filing that changes it. The Kentucky air permit summary shows the unit is the plant's main steam generator and not a backup unit, so Backup Power stays no.

### G100000402286 / Trimble County power station / 10

### G100000401618 / Cane Run Station / CR7
*question about existing data; checklist rows 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only))*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should CR7 capacity stay at 731 MW when EIA lists 807 MW?
- finding: The Louisville Air Pollution Control District statement of basis from November 2025 (https://louisvilleky.gov/sites/default/files/2025-11/20251113-basis-O-0126-25-V.pdf) rates the combined cycle unit at 731 MW. The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) lists nameplate figures of 260, 260 and 287 MW, which add to 807 MW.
- recommendation: Leave the capacity at 731 MW, which matches the permit and the existing note. Ask the project manager whether the 2024 turbine uprate should raise it toward the EIA total.
  - links: https://louisvilleky.gov/sites/default/files/2025-11/20251113-basis-O-0126-25-V.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2024-00326/rick.lovekamp%40lge-ku.com/10182024014139/06-LGE_KU_2024_IRP_Volume_I.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The 2024 integrated resource plan (https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2024-00326/rick.lovekamp%40lge-ku.com/10182024014139/06-LGE_KU_2024_IRP_Volume_I.pdf) says output is limited to 691 MW until a transmission study and network upgrades allow 697 MW in summer and 759 MW in winter, assumed for 2026. I found no document saying the limit has been lifted. The EIA table shows no planned uprate.

### G100000413046 / E.W. Brown Generating Station / 12
*question about existing data; checklist rows 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only))*
- about: other
- question: Does the 2024 LG&E/KU resource plan name Brown 12?
- finding: The plan at https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2024-00326/rick.lovekamp%40lge-ku.com/10182024014139/06-LGE_KU_2024_IRP_Volume_I.pdf adds combined cycle units without naming Brown 12 in the text I searched, and it retires Brown 3 in 2035 on the coal side.
- recommendation: Make no change from the plan. Brown 3 retirement in 2035 belongs on the coal tracker.
- notes: I searched the plan text for Brown 12 and Brown combined cycle and found only Brown 3 mentions. The commission order of October 2025 is the better source for Brown 12.

### G100001018034 / Green Station / Unit 1, timepoint 2
*question about existing data; checklist rows 38 (GEM IDs matched to EIA-860M, EIP and Sierra Club (US only))*
- about: Start year
- question: Is 2022 the right start year when EIA lists 1979 and 1981?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx, plant 6639) gives operating years 1979 for unit 1 and 1981 for unit 2. The 2026 plan at https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf says the units were converted to natural gas in 2022.
- recommendation: Leave 2022 as the start year, since it is the conversion year on a timepoint 2 row.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: EIA carries the original coal unit years. Same applies to unit 2.

### G100000402796 / J K Smith power station / GT1
*question about existing data; checklist rows 38 (GEM IDs matched to EIA-860M, EIP and Sierra Club (US only))*
- about: Capacity (MW)
- question: Should GT1 to GT3 be 119 MW each instead of 149 MW?
- finding: The EIA-860M table for September 2026 (https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx) gives 119.0 MW for GT1 to GT3 and 87.6 MW for GT4 and GT5, where GEM has 149 MW and 108 MW. The Title V air permit (https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/10125517) rates each ABB GT 11N2 turbine at 115 MW at ISO conditions, which is a different measure and not EKPC's nameplate. I found no EKPC nameplate document.
- recommendation: Change GT1 to GT3 to 119.0 MW and GT4 and GT5 to 87.6 MW on the strength of the EIA table, unless a person can get EKPC's nameplate sheet. Ask the project manager whether EIA is acceptable here.
  - links: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/xls/september_generator2026.xlsx (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/10125517 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: I looked in the harvested permit index, the Title V permit and the EKPC 2025 integrated resource plan docket summary at https://psc.ky.gov/Case/ViewCaseFilings/2025-00087. The existing resources table of the plan itself was not opened because it was not in the harvested files. EIA also gives GT6 and GT7 as 94.9 MW and GT9 and GT10 as 108.2 MW, where GEM has 98 MW for each. Those values are reported as capacity findings on those units. The EKPC 2025 plan itself was searched through the commission case page and its existing resources table could not be matched to unit names. The permit lists GT-ratings under different unit numbers, so it cannot confirm nameplates. The Kentucky Division for Air Quality Title V permit text and the commission order were read in the first pass.

### G100001071845 / KPCO 2023 IRP power station / 1 IRP
*question about existing data; checklist rows 37 (IRP box ticked on every IRP project (US only))*
- about: duplicate
- question: Should this placeholder be folded into Kentucky Power's Lawrence County combined cycle project?
- finding: The 2022 resource plan (https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2023-00092/sebishop@aep.com/03202023030104/KPCO_2022_IRP_Volume_A-Public.pdf) adds 480 MW of gas turbines in 2029. Kentucky Power later planned a 450 MW turbine (case 2026-00103), withdrew it (https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2026%20Cases/2026-00103//20261029_PSC_ORDER.pdf), and told commission staff it will file for a 760 MW combined cycle plant with a planned in-service date of March 31, 2031 (https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2026%20cases/2026-00103//20261012_PSC%20Letter%20Filing%20Informal%20Conference%20Memorandum%20and%20Attendance%20List%20into%20the%20Record.pdf). Case 2026-00324 (https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2026%20cases/2026-00324//20261030_Kentucky%20Power%20Company%20Notice%20of%20Intent%20and%20Election.pdf) is the notice of intent for a natural gas-fired combined cycle plant in Lawrence County.
- recommendation: Keep this record as announced for now. Once Kentucky Power files the application, rename or merge it into the sited Lawrence County project and change capacity to 760 MW and start year to 2031, or ask the project manager whether to do that now.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2023-00092/sebishop@aep.com/03202023030104/KPCO_2022_IRP_Volume_A-Public.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2026%20Cases/2026-00103//20261029_PSC_ORDER.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2026%20cases/2026-00103//20261012_PSC%20Letter%20Filing%20Informal%20Conference%20Memorandum%20and%20Attendance%20List%20into%20the%20Record.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscscf/2026%20cases/2026-00324//20261030_Kentucky%20Power%20Company%20Notice%20of%20Intent%20and%20Election.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The 2026-00324 application itself was not yet posted on the commission site when I looked. Only the notice of intent, the acknowledgment letter and a motion to intervene were. The site within Lawrence County is not named in what I could read. The brief suggests Big Sandy, but I found no document that says so. The 760 MW figure and the 2031 date come from commission staff's memorandum of what Kentucky Power said, not from an application. A new record for the sited plant is left for the reviewer because no site, owner entity or turbine model is yet documented.

PJM's project page names the line and the cooperative. Interconnection.fyi, which copies the PJM queue, shows 780 MW (758 MW in summer) and a planned completion at the end of 2031. Green units 1 and 2 in Webster County. The plan's text also says a related facility spans Henderson and Webster counties.
- recommendation: Change Local area from Henderson to Webster and keep City as Sebree. Leave the coordinates alone because the point is within about 300 m of the existing station and the plant stays approximate.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: No sited plant exists, since neither plan names a parcel. The 2023 staff report says the combined cycle would be built near the current station. I did not run satellite imagery or geocoding because the point does not move. Location accuracy stays approximate.

### G100000413046 / E.W. Brown Generating Station / 12
*question about existing data*
- about: other
- question: Was the Brown data center fleet check answered?
- finding: No permit in the harvest matched a data center engine fleet at Brown. The Brown Title V draft lists the combined cycle project, not a backup engine fleet.
- recommendation: Leave Backup Power as no on all units.
  - links: https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/14473287 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The draft permit lists only small existing emergency diesel engines and a fire pump, not a data center fleet. Its emergency generator engines total about 3,400 horsepower, roughly 2.6 MW, far below the 50 MW threshold for a backup generator fleet. See https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/14473287

### G100001030601 / Liberty Station Power Plant / 1
*question about existing data*
- about: other
- question: Should Backup Power stay no for Liberty Station?
- finding: The application describes a dual fuel peaking facility that sells to the grid, with diesel as an alternate fuel. No document calls the engines backup for another site.

The air permit lists an emergency generator and a fire pump engine, but these support the plant itself and are not generating units sold to the grid.

### G100000403645 / Marshall energy facility / CT1
*question about existing data*
- about: other
- question: Should Backup Power stay no for the Marshall units?
- finding: The Kentucky air permit V-22-014 from February 2023 (https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/11585453) lists eight combustion turbines and one emergency diesel fire pump engine of 221 horsepower, about 0.16 MW, and no backup generator fleet. The plant is a TVA peaking plant, not a data center.
- recommendation: Leave Backup Power as no on all eight units, now sourced to the air permit.
  - links: https://dep.gateway.ky.gov/eSearch.WebApi/api/documentGenerate/getDocument/11585453 (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: The TVA plant page https://www.tva.com/energy/our-power-system/natural-gas/marshall-combustion-turbine-plant describes the plant as a peaking facility.

- finding: The Status Detail text says coal unit 2 may run until at least 2031. EIA still lists unit 2 as operating with no planned retirement year. I made no change to the coal rows.

### G100000402523 / Robert Reid Power Plant / Reid 1, timepoint 2
*question about existing data*
- about: Notes
- question: Is the Big Rivers 2026 plan silent on a retirement year for the Reid turbine?
- finding: The 2026 Big Rivers resource plan at https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf says Big Rivers will request commission approval to retire the Reid combustion turbine, and gives no year.
- recommendation: Leave Planned retire blank. For the IRP Notes line, write that the 2026 plan seeks approval to retire the 65 MW Reid combustion turbine, out since August 2024, with no date given.
  - links: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2026-00268/senthia.santana%40bigrivers.com/09292026115438/2026_IRP_Public_Redacted_and_Appendices.pdf (ok=None, contains_value=None)
- notes: Searched the plan text for Reid and found only the retirement request, the August 2024 outage and the fuel oil backup project at Green Station that offsets its capacity.
